**Tests added** (`a0e1360`): `masked_observations_do_not_depend_on_the_withheld_frames` (evaluator: perturb the body, ball and boost of every withheld frame of a local train replay by hundreds of UU and the masked replay is byte-identical; perturbing a frame that is not withheld does change it, so the test can fail; skipped without the replay); `a_car_does_not_pick_up_a_pad_the_replay_never_reported` (train replay `0000a984`: no frame where exported boost rises by more than 50 with neither a fresh replay boost value nor a reported pickup; with the pad-blocking fix reversed it fails on exactly the frame found by the review, 3048, boost 20.1 to 99.2); `the_sealed_test_split_needs_the_final_assessment_flag` (`error_budget` guard, extracted into `sealed_path_refused`). Still without a test: the per-lifetime keys of `error_budget`, the flip-cancel refusals.

**Air schedules and held jumps (second review, suspected, now fixed).** `step_ticks` skipped an air schedule's pitch, yaw and roll on every tick where the jump control was set in the air, meant to leave a new press (a double jump or flip, whose kind RocketSim takes from the same controls) to the press itself. A held jump (up to about 0.2 s after leaving the ground, no new press) is not a press in RocketSim, so during those ticks the solved controls were dropped although `solve_air_bvp` assumed them. The skip is now limited to a rising edge (`controls.jump` and not `prev_controls.jump`). Against the LAN truth (`rlbot_reconstruction`, interior frames, old and new on the same build, four replays): air rows better or equal in all of them, small: client air rotation p90 9.15 to 9.01 deg (game 1) and 8.42 to 8.22 (game 2), air angular velocity p90 1.788 to 1.760 and 1.651 to 1.626 rad/s, all frames rotation p90 6.83 to 6.79 and 6.92 to 6.90; host 0.96 to 0.94 and 0.95 to 0.93 deg; flip rows unchanged. The held-out evaluators switch `air_bvp` off, so the validation one-step rows are identical to the printed precision and per replay (rotation p90 3.1000, angular velocity p90 0.9447 both ways).

**Dodge first-packet lag window (suspected, documented, unchanged).** `fit_dodge_start` and the flip boundary-value fit search the first packet's tick within `max(frame gap, 4)` ticks before its frame time. In every replay we have, frames are at least 4 ticks apart (3 ticks on 0.1% of frames), so the floor never matters here; for a faster replay (60 fps, 2-tick gaps) the window would exceed the gap and could pick a lag above it, unnoticed, though the fitted path is exact so only noisy packets would be at risk. Comments at both sites say so; no replay to test it on.

**Train split, and acceptance bands from the data (2026-10-02).** The held-out default and the aligned variants were run on train as well (`target/ref-final`, commit `8acdb70`; 60 of 60 convert; train 253 / 701 s, validation 264 / 731 s). Train and validation agree but differ by up to 9% at p90, the sign not the same in every row: default masked car position at horizon 1, train 17.1 / 44.6 / 76 against 16.6 / 41.0 / 75 UU; aligned masked ball 0.00 / 14.5 / 31 against 0.00 / 13.3 / 29; car angular velocity p90 1.00 against 0.95 rad/s; the one-step position and the aligned car rows within 2-4%. So the protocol's former fixed band (+10% of validation) was nearly as wide as the ordinary difference between two splits and was an arbitrary constant. `scripts/acceptance.py` replaces it: per-replay quantiles (the evaluator report now has the per-replay masked position quantiles the review found missing), the median over replays as a split's statistic, and a bootstrap prediction interval from the 120 development replays (two sources of noise: the new split's and the development estimate's). Calibration was checked by predicting one split from the other: a plain bootstrap of one split was too narrow (7 and 4 of 24 rows outside against 1.2 expected); the prediction interval gives 3 of 84 and 3 of 84 (4.2 expected). Ordering simulated < linear < hold is graded only where it holds in both development splits (30 of 32 rules; for the default masked ball at p50 and one horizon-2 case it does not). Outlier replays are listed against the largest development value of the same game size, and the former fixed time limit became a reported number.

## Review leftovers and the component audit (2026-10-02)

Branch `audit-and-leftovers` from `07e0bb6`. Baseline for paired runs: the `07e0bb6` binaries built from `git archive` (`target/baseline-07e0bb6/`); a fresh default validation run of them equals `target/ref-final/validation-default.json` in every field (0 differences, 60 replays), so the default evaluation is deterministic across builds and `ref-final` is the baseline (its `commit.txt` says `a8f900c`, a stale label: the reports contain the per-replay masked position field added in `8acdb70`, and `src` is identical between `8acdb70` and `07e0bb6`).

**Review leftovers.** (1) The edge test for a recorded jump press used the car's current controls, which already hold the frame's own jump control, so a jump held since the previous interval but not yet in the frame's controls was exported as a new press (train `0000a984`: the car had been jumping for 11 ticks). It now compares with the jump control of the previous interval's last tick (`jump_press_ticks`, unit test). On 12 train replays (four per game size) the `jump` rows of `fitted_inputs` fall from 2,626 to 2,603 (-0.9%, nine replays differ); no other output field changes. (2) The pad pickup counter and the dodge-refresh counter were already cleared when an actor is deleted; the demolition repeat window now is too (a recycled car id used to inherit the 5 s window). Extracted observations (`hash_observations`, the full serialized observations including event repeat flags) hash identically on all 120 train and validation replays, so the corpus has no such recycled id. Unit tests cover the demolition and pad cases. (3) The per-frame median car lag already follows moved runs (commit `5c75b5d`); that note was stale.

**Audit, part 1: lag inference chains, `observations.rs`, dodge-refresh application** (reviewer, reading plus scans on 8 train replays). No defect that corrupts the outputs. Findings:
1. A car packet at a run boundary belongs to two runs: when a pair (a, b) cannot extend the current run, the run ending at `a` is finished and a new run `[a, b]` starts, so `a` gets the later, two-packet run's lag in `car_actor`/`car_run_of` and both lags enter the per-frame median. 1,857 of 127,161 car packets (1.5%) on 8 train replays, all with different lags (1 tick 1,220; 2 355; 3 227; 4 55). Which choice is better is open; to be measured.
2. `actor_slots` (the car-id-to-slot map used by the observed demolition and dodge-refresh application) is keyed by actor id only, so a shadowed older car of the same player could reach the replacement's slot. Never observed.
3. An observed dodge refresh is applied after the frame's interval is simulated, so a flip in the same frame still sees the old flags. No such case in the 10 train increments.
4. `bridge_ok` checks the frames after `a` and `b` but not `a`; a withheld frame can keep a real packet (a car created in it, or the ball when the previous frame has none). No metric effect found.
5. The demolition `repeat` flag is a heuristic: the replay's own re-reports come 1.7-4.4 s after the first, and a genuine re-demolition can come 3 s after a respawn, so no window separates them.

**Audit, part 2: Python loaders against the Rust writers** (train 3v3 `000a9939`). `load_numpy` (JSONL) equals `load_columnar_numpy` (Parquet) in every array; the Python writer matches the Rust writer; all 7 record tables match the JSONL cell by cell (209 touches, 233 ball contacts, 631 boost pickups, 9,522 fitted inputs, 38,234 packet lags, 21 events, 2,467 pad pickups); the merged Parquet header equals the JSONL header. Findings:
1. Record tables from an earlier export are returned by `read_record_tables` next to a new main file (reproduced with `--no-event-tables`); the tables carry no replay hash.
2. `events`, `packet_lags` and `pad_pickups` name cars by replay actor id, and no typed output maps an actor id to a car slot (only `frame_json` does).
3. The column listing in "Parquet columns and record tables" predates `fitted_inputs` kind `air` and `span_ticks`, `packet_lags` source `dodge_fit`, and `events` kind `dodge_refreshed` (columns `car`, `refreshed_count`; the JSON key is `count`); README's `.to_pandas()` examples need pandas, which `requirements-columnar.txt` does not pin.
4. `convert_replay` writes raw JSONL for any extension other than `.parquet`, including `.jsonl.gz`, which the Python reader then tries to decompress.
5. No automated test covers the record tables, multi-row-group reads or absent car slots.
Suspected: never-replicated throttle, steer or handbrake are 0 in the typed control columns with no mask (in `000a9939` handbrake is unreplicated on 9,333 of 66,447 linked car-frames; throttle-unobserved frames with a fresh velocity are all slower than 300 UU/s, consistent with neutral), and inferred match-start zero scores look like reported zeros outside `frame_json`.

**Audit, part 3: the tools behind the protocol's numbers** (reviewer; train only). The evaluator arithmetic is sound: simulated, hold and linear are scored on the same samples (equal counts in every row), the mask schedule and aggregation do what the protocol says, and `check_scoreboard` reproduces 98.4% on train. Findings, all about what a number means or whether the frozen run can produce it:
1. **The masked "horizon" is the withheld frame's offset in the mask window, not the age of the packet the prediction starts from.** A masked car starts from its last fresh body, often older than the frame before the window (cars are not refreshed every frame). A replication of the schedule reproduces the report's sample counts exactly (car 10,120 / 9,931 / 9,877 / 10,053 at h1-h4); the start-packet age p50 is 0.067 / 0.069 / 0.103 / 0.167 s for cars (ball exactly 0.033 / 0.067 / 0.100 / 0.133 s). Only 1,854 of 10,120 car samples at h1 start from the frame before the window. So car "1 frame ahead" is about two frames, car h1 and h2 span the same time, the car error is not monotonic in the label (simulated p50 17.1, 15.6, 16.4, 16.6 UU on train), and ball and car rows at the same label are not comparable in time. Comparisons between runs and the simulated/hold/linear ordering are unaffected (all start from the same packet). The labels need to say this, and the reports should carry the age.
2. `check_scoreboard` and `count_demolitions` refuse any path containing "test", so the protocol's section 3a commands would abort at the frozen run (the defect fixed for `error_budget`).
3. The demolition rows of `TEST_PROTOCOL.md` section 5 are stale: the code's repeat window is 5 s, not 3 s; the current train extraction has 298 non-goal demolition events, 84 flagged repeats and 22 whose victim has no linked car (silently dropped by `count_demolitions`); and "0 simulated" holds by construction because RocketSim's own demolition rule is off by default (`NO_OBSERVED_DEMOS` gives the informative comparison).
4. `rlbot_reconstruction` scores only fitted presses matched to a true press within 30 ticks, so its press error quantiles are on the matched subset (LAN game 1 client: 228 jumps and 299 dodges matched, against 596 and 457 true events in the whole recording, before removing those outside active play). The client jump row quoted in "Fitted presses in the export" (231, 1 / 4) is the "no dodge first-packet inference" block; the "all fits" block gives 228, 1 / 3.
5. `scripts/acceptance.py` builds the stated bootstrap prediction band, but: the rows are strongly correlated, so "about 4 of 84 outside by chance" (binomial) is not the right yardstick (300 pseudo-splits from train: mean 1.2, p95 4, max 10 outside); per-replay masked p99 is close to the maximum (median 90 samples per replay, nearest-rank quantiles); missing or NaN rows are skipped silently; the one-step outliers are listed twice.
6. Several section 5 rows (pickups matched to RocketSim's list, pickups whose path reaches the pad, simulated touches in a contact interval, lag-free replays) have no command in section 3a.
Suspected: `rlbot_reconstruction` drops frames where either side is demolished; 155 of 109,228 car-frames in the train mask windows (0.14%) belong to a car created inside the window and keep its real packet (part 1, finding 4).

**Fixes from parts 1 and 2** (no change to the default physics: default JSONL of train `0000a984`, `00054e5d` and `000a9939` is byte-identical to the `0e4972d` build, and the evaluator reports of `0000a984` and `000a9939` are identical). Observed demolitions and dodge refreshes now require the car to be a player-linked car of the frame with the same lifetime (part 1, finding 2); `bridge_ok` also refuses a withheld first frame (finding 4). The record tables carry `source_sha256` and `frames` in their metadata and `read_record_tables` skips a table that does not match the main file (the audit's reproduction is now skipped with a warning); `convert_replay --no-event-tables` deletes stale tables and other output extensions are refused (there is no gzip dependency, so `.jsonl.gz` is refused too). `events`, `packet_lags` and `pad_pickups` gained nullable slot columns (`ConvertedFrame::car_actor_slots`, resolved through the car's linked player). On train `000a9939` the resolved slot's car is near the observed actor: demolition attackers p50 / p90 / max 19 / 61 / 76 UU (9), dodge refreshes 3 / 18 / 24 (6), pad instigators 45 / 115 / 230 (925 of 936; 11 unlinked cars have no slot), packet lags 19 / 54 / 96 (29,060); 8 of 9 demolition victims are within 44 UU and the ninth is a repeat report for a shadowed car already replaced (its player's slot, the replacement's pose). `python/verify_direct_parquet.py` now compares all seven tables row by row with the JSONL (null mapping per kind, `count` to `refreshed_count`, float32, slot columns, metadata) and passes on `000a9939`.

**Audit, part 4: the offline fits, and whether anything after a masked window reaches a prediction in it** (reviewer; train 3v3 `00059a28`, 1v1 `000cf226` and `00a0da63`, 2v2 `00a02f8f`). Method: the masked conversion replicated with the evaluator's options, the exported states of frames W-60..W+3 compared after (i) perturbing every packet after the window, (ii) perturbing the 30 frames before it (control), (iii) truncating the replay right after the window, (iv) negating the observed controls inside or after it. The fits themselves are clean: every fit refuses a withheld frame between the car's packet and each packet it reads, and exact chains are broken at withheld frames. Findings:
1. **The aligned masked variant is not causal: truncating the replay after a window changes predictions inside it, through the replay-wide ball-car offset estimate** (`estimate_ball_car_offset` / `place_ball_runs`; defined on 13 of 60 train replays). 1v1 `00a0da63`: 12 of 26 sampled windows differ, per-window max position difference p50 0.0, p90 11.0, max 18.5 UU (2v2 `00a02f8f` window 801: 16.1 UU), with the offset off the differences vanish; the default variant and the replays without an offset are bit-identical. `TEST_PROTOCOL.md` listed this as limit 8 and called it small; at 5-18 UU it is the size of the aligned rows' p90 (16.8 UU).
2. Pad state in a window depends on later pickups through the replay-wide pad-name votes (3v3 `00059a28` window 801: two pads' cooldowns and activity differ in every window frame, both variants); no position effect seen. It reaches the masked boost rows.
3. The masked predictor is conditional on the true observed controls of the withheld frames (negating them changes window predictions by 0 / 1-2.5 / 4-7 / 8-33 UU at h1-h4), and in the aligned variant the ground-control lookahead and negative timing shifts also carry them into the interval before the window (earliest differing frame W-3 to W-1). Controls after the window change nothing. This is a premise (controls are inputs), but the protocol does not state it.
4. `plan_air_bvp` takes a packet's end tick from the chain lags and never from `lag_overrides`, so for the packet after a dodge (lag fitted by the dodge-start or ground-flip fit) the solve aims at a different tick than the main loop injects. Patched copy, LAN game 1 client (`rlbot_reconstruction --thin 2`): flip rows rotation p90 20.63 to 20.48 deg, angular velocity p90 3.192 to 3.166 rad/s, air/flip rows never worse; export only (the evaluators turn `air_bvp` off).
5. Small: `past_persisted_air_controls` reads the next frame's timestamp (a timestamp only; scored positions unchanged); `boost_pickup_lookahead` (off by default, env `BOOST_LOOKAHEAD` in `rlbot_reconstruction`) has no withheld guard.
Suspected: fits copy `last_extra_hit_tick` from the main arena into a scratch arena with an unrelated tick counter, which can switch RocketSim's hit-impulse cooldown (`ball/base.rs:264`) in scratch ball contacts; a NaN solve passes the `plan_air_bvp` tolerance test (`>` comparisons); the ground-control fit's parked scratch ball at (0, 0, 1800) is not moved away from a ceiling car.

**Review of the committed audit fixes, and part 5: restoration** (reviewer). `0e4972d` and `a4f77da` are sound (default output unchanged, `verify_direct_parquet.py` passes on `000a9939`, no test that cannot fail). Open from them: the record tables' `source_sha256` and `frames` are only in the Parquet footer, which PyArrow's schema metadata does not show (the Rust test passes only because arrow-rs merges the footer into the schema; `read_record_tables` reads the footer, so the check works); a truncated table makes `read_record_tables` raise instead of skipping it; provenance does not cover the conversion options; under `lag_boundary` `earlier`/`longer` the ball-car offset is estimated on the full runs but applied to the owned entries. Restoration: the detached restore maps every public car and ball field and `verify_state_restoration` passes (11,025 exact snapshots on `000a9939`), but **`apply_soccar_state_to_arena` copies the absolute `last_extra_hit_tick` into a live arena with a different tick counter**, and RocketSim grants the extra hit impulse only when `last_hit_tick + 1 < tick_count` (`ball/base.rs:264` at `0b02051`). A car with `Some(5000)` seeded into an arena at tick 0 sends a resting ball off at 999 UU/s instead of 1,791 UU/s (the same hit with `None`, with `Some(5)`, or in an arena at tick 6000). The verifier cannot see it (it overwrites the live tick and compares the copied field) and README does not mention it. The same copy happens from the main arena into the offline fits' scratch arenas (part 4, suspected item), so the effect there is of this size whenever a fit simulates a ball contact soon after a real hit and the scratch tick counter is below the main one. Minor: missing optional record fields (`world_contact_normal`, `last_extra_hit_tick`) restore as None without error; `restoration::hitbox` duplicates `conversion::hitbox_config`.

**Fixes from part 3** (no conversion output change; every earlier field of the `evaluate_corpus` report of train `0000a984` is identical). Masked rows carry `start_packet_age_seconds` (p10/p50/p90; pooled, per game size, per replay) and the report has `masked_position_uu_by_start_packet_age_bucket` (all horizons pooled by age: <=0.04, <=0.08, <=0.12, <=0.20, >0.20 s; ball and car; simulated, hold, linear), printed by `summarize_reference.py` next to each label (`0000a984`: car age p50 0.067 / 0.100 / 0.100 / 0.167 s at h1-h4, ball 0.033 / 0.067 / 0.100 / 0.133). `sealed_path_refused` is shared (`src/lib.rs`) and `error_budget`, `check_scoreboard`, `count_demolitions` and the new `consistency_counts` take `--final-assessment`. `count_demolitions` on train: 298 non-goal events = 197 linked, not repeats + 84 repeats (5 s window) + 17 with no linked victim; 248 post-goal explosions excluded; simulated demolitions are reported as not counted while RocketSim's rule is off (`NO_OBSERVED_DEMOS=1` turns it on). `rlbot_reconstruction` prints, per press kind, fitted / fitted without a true press within 30 ticks / true in active play / true without a fit: LAN game 1 client, all fits, jumps 228 / 0 / 575 / 357, dodges 301 / 2 / 439 / 135 (so the press error quantiles describe 38% of true jumps and 69% of true dodges). `scripts/acceptance.py` adds an empirical null for the number of rows outside the bands (train: mean 1.0, p95 4, p99 6, max 9 of 84 over 300 pseudo-splits), per-row replay counts, a message for every skipped row, null-quantile guards and a deduplicated outlier list; the validation reports give 0 of 84 outside. `consistency_counts` (train, 60 replays): 0 lag-free; 23,370 boost pickups, pad matched 100.0%, car path reaches the pad 96.1%; 11,955 simulated touches, 96.8% inside a ball-contact interval; 13,760 contact intervals, 85.1% holding a simulated touch; scoreboard integer equals the ceiling of the reconstruction on 98.4% of running frames, off by one 1.6%, by more 0.

## Outside reviews by Codex and Gemini: triage (2026-10-02)

The user had Codex and Gemini review the project; their reports (`CODEX_REVIEW.md` at `205dede` plus the uncommitted batch 5, `GEMINI_REVIEW.md`) are local files, not committed. The reviewer checked every finding against the code (HEAD `205dede`; scans and reproductions on train only) and classed it as known, verified, refuted or unverified, applying the rule that dependence on future frames is by design and only use of a scored or withheld packet is a defect.

**Verified, and worth fixing before the freeze:**
* Codex C16: `verify_state_restoration` compares the live `last_extra_hit_tick` with the serialized absolute value, so it fails once the batch 5 rebase is in (fixed with batch 5).
* Codex C09: a fresh *sleeping* rigid-body packet leaves the simulated velocity in place. 537 fresh sleeping car packets on train, 405 in active play; in 8 replays the exported speed at such frames is p50 1,869 UU/s while the raw pose stays frozen for 30+ frames, and the one-step position error there is p50 114 UU.
* Not in either review: before a car's first rigid-body packet its exported state is RocketSim's default (0, 0, 17) with 33.3 boost: 3,093 of 2,720,955 primary-car frames on train (0.11%); boxcars' spawn trajectory is not used.
* Codex C14: a failed export truncates an existing output (an 11.9 MB Parquet file to 0 bytes; `--no-event-tables` deletes the tables before a parse failure).
* Codex C04: only the game-type string is checked, so non-standard maps or mutators would be simulated as standard (train: mutator index always -1; one Throwback `Stadium_10A` replay converts with normal residuals). The original plan required detection.
* Codex C28: an existing but empty mesh directory makes RocketSim panic instead of returning an error.
* Codex C20: `acceptance.py` joins the default and aligned reports by path without checking the replay hashes, options or the replay count.
* Codex C35: `sealed_path_refused` is case-sensitive and `evaluate_corpus` and the audit binaries lack it.
* Codex C19: `--air-bvp` or `--fit-on-next-packet` on `evaluate_corpus` still labels the one-step rows held out.
* Codex C26: `scripts/run_reference.sh` copies an existing binary without building and ends `ALL-DONE` whatever the exit codes.
* Contact alignment (Codex C10, C11, C12, C32; Gemini BUG-01, BUG-02, EDGE-01): the candidate car takes the first car with the player's key rather than the primary one, starts from a default `CarState` with `z < 30` as the ground rule and default boost, the ball is parked at (0, 0, 1800) in an arena with live pads, and contact poses take the later rotation instead of interpolating. Effects unmeasured.
* Codex C05: the main loop does not call RocketSim's `refresh_car_sticky_gate` after injecting car states, as `arena/base.rs:781-799` asks; a patched copy changes one-step car velocity p90 from 37.76 to 37.77 UU/s on two train replays (no effect, still worth the line).

**Verified mechanisms with zero incidence on the 60 train replays** (hardening): a player key that changes allocates a new slot (C01: 0 changes in 240 players); a departed player's car keeps simulating (C02: 0); a replacement car linked after its creation is treated as the old lifetime (C03: 0 of 1,870 lifetimes); a deleted PRI leaves cars pointing at a reusable id (C07: 0); a late loadout or team change never updates the slot (C08: 0); the overtime clock without a visible change is held at 0 (C13: 0 of 12,457 overtime frames affected); zero car slots make the Parquet writer fail (C15); backward frame times are clamped silently (C27: 0); the rebase drops a hit one tick before a tick-0 restore (C06).

**Minor, verified:** the Parquet benchmark script cannot parse the multi-line stdout (C25); the legacy non-exact lag mode leaves the run registries empty (C30, Gemini BUG-04); provenance skips `withheld_frames` and `external_packet_lags` (C18) and a main file without `options_sha256` skips the option check (C17); rotation traces use the raw packet while the aligned aggregate uses the aligned target (C22); the network parse uses boxcars' default CRC policy (C36); the air forward model omits RocketSim's int8 input quantization (`car/base.rs`, at most 1/128 of the control; Codex, Gemini BUG-07); ball-contact preparation errors are dropped with `.ok()`; contact alignment's snapshot copies cost +17 MB (13%) on an 11,025-frame 3v3 replay (P01); allocation-heavy spots in the Parquet path (P02-P04, Gemini OPT-01/02), unmeasured.

**Already known or documented:** the null of `acceptance.py` (C21, being rebuilt), the ball-car offset search bounds (C31, Gemini BUG-03), terminal expiry as `decided` (C34), the exported-controls contract, the 5 s demolition heuristic, equal baseline sample sets, retained body IDs (Gemini HYG-02).

**Refuted:** Gemini BUG-05 (the newest car lifetime winning is intended; the old one is the dead car), BUG-06 (the LM normal matrix is positive definite with pivots at least 0.0025), HYG-01 (`Sink::create` is used in production). Unverified: the RLBot one-step tool's missing `prev_controls` and live pads (C23, C24), legacy loadout and pickup layouts (C29), and some input validation of internal helpers.

## Packet shared by two lag runs: keep the earlier run's lag (2026-10-02)

Audit part 1, finding 1: when a pair (a, b) cannot extend an exact lag chain, a new run starts at `a`, so `a` is in two runs whose lags differ by 1-4 ticks (often more for clients); the later, two-packet run overwrote it and both lags entered the car's per-frame median. Experimental option `lag_boundary` (`0e4972d`): `later` (old), `earlier` (the run that ended at `a` keeps it, counted once), `longer` (the run with more packets).

**Train** (evaluator, `0e4972d` built from `git archive`; `later` equals `target/ref-final` in both variants): the default variant is unchanged by construction (its masked predictor does not infer lags; masked rows bit-identical, one-step rows within noise, car position p90 3.81 UU under all three). Aligned masked car position, `later` to `earlier`: h1 0.618 / 16.85 / 56.9 to 0.564 / 16.41 / 55.3 UU, h4 3.72 / 19.40 / 66.4 to 3.54 / 19.28 / 66.3; per replay, h1 p90 improves in 27 of 60, worsens in 1 (3v3 `00059a28`, +0.56 UU). `longer` gives about half the gain. The aligned targets use the same rule (the aligned linear baseline moves by 0.1-0.4 UU too), so part of this is predictor/target consistency; hence the truth check.

**LAN truth** (client and host replays of both LAN games; `dump_replay_packets` with the rule in a local copy, scored with the matching of `scripts/rlbot_lag_check.py`): host replays have no boundary packets. On the clients, all matched packets: car links exact 91.6-91.9% and ball 96.2-96.7% under every rule. At the boundary packets (game 1: 44 car, 99 ball; game 2: 57 car, 39 ball) a packet joins two internally exact runs whose levels disagree, so one of its two links is wrong whatever it keeps: `later` keeps the outgoing link (95-96% exact, incoming 0-9%), `earlier` the incoming one (98%, outgoing 0-7%). Against the floor-based level truth `earlier` is closer on 33 of 44 (game 1) and 39 of 57 (game 2) boundary car packets, mean absolute level error 4.0 / 4.4 ticks against 9.1 / 8.8 for `later` (whose per-frame median of the two run lags is biased by about -9 ticks); `longer` is no better than `later` (9.5 / 9.3). Ball boundary packets: `earlier` closer in 6 vs 2 and 13 vs 6 of those that differ.

**Validation, aligned, `earlier` against `target/ref-final`** (60 replays, sample counts unchanged): masked car position h1 0.564 / 16.49 / 53.8 to 0.522 / 15.90 / 52.3 UU, h2 0.856 / 15.85 / 45.1 to 0.812 / 15.45 / 43.4, h3 1.94 / 17.96 / 55.8 to 1.86 / 17.68 / 54.7, h4 3.16 / 19.17 / 57.8 to 3.07 / 19.12 / 57.6; p50 and p90 better at every horizon and game size, p99 better except 1v1 h1 (69.0 to 70.3, n 1,560). Ball p90 13.33 to 13.10 at h1, no regression above 0.1 UU. Per replay, h1 p90 improves in 33 of 60 and worsens in none (best `1v1/1a3ac92c` 26.85 to 19.17). One-step rows within noise.

**Decision:** `earlier` becomes the default; `later` stays as an ablation, `longer` is removed (dominated). Open idea, untested: re-anchor the whole following run at a junction so both links stay exact.

## Audit fix batch 5: tick rebase, `plan_air_bvp` lag overrides, truncation leak test, table metadata, a disjoint null (2026-10-02)

* **Hit ticks across arenas.** Every scratch-arena seeding of the offline fits (`plan_air_bvp`, ground, jump, flip-timing, flip-cancel and dodge-start fits) rebases `last_extra_hit_tick` into the scratch arena's tick counter (`rebase_tick`, `seed_scratch_car`; states restored from a recorded scratch path rebase from the tick they were recorded at); `apply_soccar_state_to_arena` rebases the car's and the dropshot ball's tick fields. Unit test with the reviewer's probe: without the rebase a recent hit seeded into another arena gives 946 instead of 1,646 UU/s. `verify_state_restoration` now checks the live arena at ticks 0 and 1,000 against the rebased value (`000a9939`: 11,025 exact snapshots, 6 live samples, pad error 0). On three train replays the scratch rebase changes no exported frame.
* **`plan_air_bvp` aims at the injected tick** (end lag from `lag_overrides` first). Export only (the evaluators turn `air_bvp` off): default JSONL changes in 967 of 11,663 (`0000a984`), 1,746 of 10,935 (`00054e5d`) and 3,570 of 11,025 (`000a9939`) frames, in air-segment car physics, flip state, controls and air rows (`fitted_inputs` of `000a9939` 9,522 to 9,582 rows). LAN game 1 client (`rlbot_reconstruction --thin 2`): flip rows rotation p90 20.63 to 20.48 deg, angular velocity p90 3.192 to 3.166 rad/s, `is_flipping` mismatches 773 to 760.
* **Truncation leak test** (`masked_conversions_do_not_depend_on_frames_after_a_window`, `evaluate_corpus`, train 1v1 `00a0da63`): the default masked predictor is unchanged in physics and boost when the replay is cut right after windows 401 and 901 (pads differ in 361 frames: the pad-name votes); a ball packet from 40 frames later fed into frame 401 changes 4 frames (negative control). The aligned predictor is printed, not asserted: 126 of 905 frames differ, largest position difference 28.9 UU (the replay-wide ball-car offset, by design). The report lists the masked runs' option overrides (`masked_run_options`).
* Small: `boost_pickup_lookahead` refuses a withheld next frame; a NaN `plan_air_bvp` solve is refused; the ground-control fit and contact alignment move the parked ball away from a car near (0, 0, 1800) (no change on three replays); `evaluate_corpus` has the sealed-path guard with `--final-assessment`.
* **Record tables:** `source_sha256` and `options_sha256` (of the serialized `ConvertOptions`) in each table's schema metadata (visible to `pq.read_table`), `frames` in the footer; `read_record_tables` checks all three and skips an unreadable or truncated table with a warning.
* **Acceptance null from disjoint partitions.** The 120 development replays are split at random into A and B (stratified by size, 60 partitions); bands are built on A with the `bands` code and the rows outside are counted on B. With 20 per size in each: B itself (a fresh 60-replay split disjoint from the bands) mean 5.7, p95 11 of 84 rows; pseudo-splits drawn from B with replacement (which roughly doubles a split's variance) mean 10.5, p95 19. B itself is the comparable null for a test split. The real cross-split counts are 0 (train bands, validation) and 3 (validation bands, train).

**Batch 5 measured on train** (evaluator, `f9e2c9f` from `git archive`, default options, against `target/ref-final`; 60 of 60 convert, sample counts unchanged). Masked position rows are unchanged in both variants (default bit-identical; aligned to the printed precision, no replay's masked p90 moves at any horizon). One-step car position 0.053 / 3.81 / 37.5 UU unchanged, car velocity p90 47.28 to 47.27 UU/s, ball velocity p99 396.3 to 396.6; some per-replay masked kinematics tails of small n move (e.g. `1v1/0000a984` masked ball velocity p99 at h1). Determinism: the aligned run of `1v1/00a0da63` gives identical reports over five runs (parallel and sequential), and its masked ball p90 is 11.52 / 16.92 / 17.88 / 17.56 at h1-h4 on `ref-final`, `fc8cdbf`, `0e4972d` and `f9e2c9f`; the 12.97 / 16.09 seen during batch 5 came from an uncommitted working-tree state and does not reproduce. Reviewer notes on batch 5: the restoration verifier checks the rebased tick with the production `rebase_tick`, so the semantics rest on the unit test (the full extra impulse after seeding); the age-one hit at a tick-0 destination is documented, not tested.

## Audit fix batch 6, parts 1-2: `earlier` default, sleeping packets, spawn poses, contact alignment, sticky gate (2026-10-02)

Default JSONL against `f9e2c9f` on train `0000a984` / `00054e5d` / `000a9939` (each item measured on top of the previous):
* **`lag_boundary` earlier is the default** (`longer` removed, `later` an ablation flag); the ball-car offset estimate, `median_hit_gap` and `place_ball_runs` all see the owned packets. 1,207 / 4,118 / 2,422 frames change (the counts of the earlier experiment).
* **Sleeping packets** (Codex C09): a fresh rigid-body packet with `sleeping` set and no fresh velocity zeroes the simulated linear and angular velocity (a velocity in the same packet wins); the observations are unchanged, so the omitted velocity stays unknown; frames list it in `sleeping_velocity_inferred`, diagnostics count `sleeping_car_packets` / `sleeping_ball_packets`. Zeroed packets cars / ball 6 / 9, 13 / 5, 8 / 6; frames changed 1,098 / 1,259 / 975 (ball changes post-goal, car changes mostly in active play). The reviewer's example (`0000a984` actor 33, frames 2724-2757, a goal-explosion victim no longer linked) still moves at about 2,185 UU/s, because the frozen car overlaps the ball and RocketSim pushes it; holding such a body out of the simulation is open.
* **Cars before their first rigid-body packet** start from boxcars' spawn trajectory (location and heading; rotation skipped when the trajectory tilts the car), with zero velocity, once per lifetime (`cars_started_from_spawn_trajectory` 18 / 32 / 32); the observed car carries `spawn_pose` until its first packet. On train the spawn location agrees with the first packet to p90 3 UU, max 10.1 UU (kickoff spawns exact); exported pre-packet frames were p50 3,849 UU from the first packet before and about 1 UU after; 15 / 53 / 21 frames change. Every car actor had a spawn location.
* **Contact alignment** uses the player's primary car, starts the candidate from the first-pass exported car state (wheel contacts, ground state, boost, flags, rebased hit tick) with the packet physics, skips demolished cars, slerps the contact pose's rotation and refuses a pose across a lifetime change: 618 / 571 / 165 frames change.
* **`refresh_car_sticky_gate`** after every car-state injection (main loop and scratch seeding): 2,490 / 4,721 / 3,878 frames change, almost all negligibly (car position p50 0.000 UU, p90 0.098 / 0.000 UU; velocity p90 1.3 / 0.0 UU/s), tails up to 19.2 UU and 509 UU/s.
* Review follow-ups in the same commit: `fit_flip_cancel`'s later targets seed from the scratch tick (only with `flip_cancel_packets > 1`); the leak test truncates right after the window and asserts the aligned predictor's known dependence as a positive control (126 of 905 frames, 28.9 UU) besides the default predictor's independence; `plan_air_bvp` clamps the lag to the frame gap (no frame changes); `Sink::create` test-only; a Python test for the options-hash mismatch; `acceptance.py` gives its verdict against the "B itself" null (p95 11, p99 13, max 14 of 84 rows) and reads older bands files with a note.

**Dead pawn shells and review follow-ups** (reviewer's check of `578d077`). All 405 fresh sleeping car packets in active play on train belong to cars whose pawn link is inactive: dead pawn shells, which the simulation kept moving (after the batch 6 zeroing, 15 of 58 non-demoed shells on 10 replays still moved above 100 UU/s, one-step error p50 101 UU) and which the ball and live cars could hit. A goal-explosion demolition event (observed; it was skipped before) or a sleeping packet of a car with no active link (inferred, listed in `demolition_inferred`) now holds the car demolished until the slot's next lifetime or its next live packet, not for RocketSim's 3 s; never in a withheld frame; a car that merely sleeps with an active link is never touched (unit test). Diagnostics `goal_explosion_demolitions`, `dead_shells_inferred`, `dead_shells_released`. On `0000a984` / `00054e5d` / `000a9939` 354 / 807 / 493 frames change; on 12 train replays 2,602 shell car-frames are held, of which 2,049 moved above 100 UU/s before and none after; the ball's one-step residual within 10 frames of them is unchanged (p99 23.87 to 23.84 UU, max 73.2), so the gain is in the car states, not the ball. Also: under `earlier` the offset estimate looks hits up in the unowned ball runs again (it had lost 0-4 of 36-96 usable hits; `00a0da63` 78 of 78, offset unchanged), `sleeping_velocity_inferred` lists every fresh sleeping packet including those already at rest, and the sticky gate is refreshed after contact alignment's teleports and in `apply_soccar_state_to_arena` (no output change). Reviewer checks that passed: spawn heading against the first packet over 1,415 lifetimes p50 0.0 deg, max 3.3 deg; the contact-alignment seeding and slerp; the leak test and the "B itself" verdict (complementary halves give two 60-replay statistics the variance of two independent splits).

**Robustness round** (default output unchanged: frames byte-identical to `bb48d6e` on three train replays, only new header diagnostics). Atomic export (temporary names, published on success; stale tables removed only after success; checked with a failing run over an existing output). Map and mutator diagnostics (`map_name`, the announced `ReplicatedGameMutatorIndex` and ball gravity/speed scales, `nonstandard_notes`): train has 18 maps (`EuroStadium_Night_P` 21, `Stadium_P` 7, ... one Throwback `STADIUM_10A_P`), mutator index -1 in the 30 replays that announce it, no scale attributes, 0 notes. A clean error for a mesh directory without soccar meshes. `acceptance.py` checks that default and aligned reports cover the same replays (path and SHA-256), the same options, no failures and the expected count (60). `sealed_path_refused` is case-insensitive on the resolved path. `--air-bvp` and `--fit-on-next-packet` label the one-step rows `offline-fits`. `run_reference.sh` builds first and ends `FAILED` on any failed run; `evaluate_corpus` exits non-zero when a replay fails. Counters (train incidence): player key changes 0, players deleted with cars 0 (their cars' links are now cleared), replacement cars linked after creation 0 of 1,630 replacements, slot loadout changes, non-monotonic frame times 0, `ball_interval_error`. The Parquet writer refuses a replay with zero car slots; the legacy non-exact lag chains are refused together with contact alignment or the ball-car offset; `convert_replay` rejects unknown dash options.

**Batch 6 measured** (evaluator; baseline `f9e2c9f` with `earlier`, so the lag rule is factored out). `578d077` regressed the LAN game 1 client ball (all frames 5.92 / 20.1 / 53 to 7.96 / 20.7 / 64 UU; away from cars p50 0.01 to 6.69) and mildly game 2: with `NO_EST_MU` the builds are identical, so it was the ball-car offset estimate, which had lost hits under `earlier` (game 1 offset 2.7726 to 2.7484, game 2 3.3151 to 2.9820). `bb48d6e` restores both offsets exactly and the ball rows to the baseline; car rows differ by 1-2 frames; hosts unchanged. Train with `bb48d6e`: pooled rows within 0.02 UU at p50 and 0.05 at p90 in both variants; one-step ball velocity p99 395.1 to 386.7 UU/s, car velocity p90 47.32 to 47.18. The two largest per-replay outliers of `578d077` are explained and gone: `1v1/00a3439b` (default masked car h4 p90 38.5 to 52.4) was one window in which the predicted car hit a frozen opponent shell 70 UU away and went airborne; with the shell held demolished the prediction stays on the ground (error 14.6 UU at h4, p90 back to 38.5); `3v3/000e1a16` (aligned masked ball) was the offset estimate (with it off, all builds agree; with it on, this replay's masked ball p90 is 11.5-14.9 UU against 9.1-11.2 without, so a weakly constrained offset of about 0.2 tick can hurt). Still worse by more than 2% at p90 on train: `2v2/00b3b6e3` masked ball h3 (+11% default, +8% aligned; no offset estimated there, not contact alignment or ball chains), `2v2/00a02f8f` aligned masked ball h3 (+17%), `3v3/001a5f59` aligned masked car h1 (+13%); two car one-step samples dropped (998,821 to 998,819).

**The remaining train outliers of `bb48d6e`, frame by frame** (evaluator; scratch ball traces in copies of the source). (1) `2v2/00b3b6e3` default masked ball h3 (+11%) is one window (frames 2201-2204): a respawned car (actor 80, created at 2199, first packet at 2201) is started at its spawn pose with zero velocity, inside contact range of the ball it had really hit between 2199 and 2200; the source ball packet at 2200 already holds the post-hit velocity, so the masked simulation scores a second touch (ball h3 error 0.26 to 63 UU). The spawn pose is right; letting a car known only from its spawn pose collide before its first packet is wrong (being fixed). (2) The two dropped car one-step samples (`3v3/00054e5d` frame 9806, `3v3/001964e4` frame 1499) are fresh sleeping packets of resting dead cars that the baseline simulated at a stale ~1,360 UU/s (errors 679 and 97 UU); now held demolished and skipped, as intended. (3) `2v2/00a02f8f` aligned masked ball h3 (+17%): the prediction is identical; the aligned target moved one tick because the masked conversions' offset estimate changed (0.391 to 0.435 ticks) and a ball run's placement crossed a rounding boundary; which placement is right is not decidable on train. (4) `3v3/001a5f59` aligned masked car h1 (+13%): one window where a car's first ticks after a jump-start packet go wrong (vx 414 against 38 UU/s) only when a dead shell on another slot, about 100 UU away and at rest, is held demolished; moving the shell far away does not restore it, so it is not a contact; mechanism under investigation.

**Two output fixes from the outliers** (also in commit `c5b85b9`, whose message names only the provenance and guard changes). (1) A car known only from its spawn pose (no rigid-body packet in its lifetime yet) takes no part in collisions until its first packet: RocketSim has no per-car collision switch, only the demolished state (`car/base.rs` `pre_tick_update` sets a demoed car `DisableSimulation | NoContactResponse`), so such a car is kept demolished internally with the timer re-armed each frame while the export shows the spawn pose and `is_demoed` false, marked per frame in JSONL `spawn_pose_held`. Unit test. `2v2/00b3b6e3` window 2201-2204: ball error 24.9 / 62.9 / 63.3 / 86.2 to 9.1 / 27.4 / 0.3 / 0.3 UU (the baseline's values); 10 / 23 / 0 frames of the three test replays carry `spawn_pose_held`. (2) The `3v3/001a5f59` regression was ours, not RocketSim's: the dead shell's sleeping packet arrived with the report of its own demolition by the neighbouring car, and holding it demolished from the packet's tick removed the bump of that demolition, which in the replay slows the attacker (vx 301 to 38 UU/s; simulated 414). A shell whose demolition is reported in the same frame is now held only after that frame's interval (`observed`; counter `dead_shells_after_demolition`, 5 on `000a9939`). Unit test. Its aligned masked car h1 p90 13.8 to 12.3 UU, p99 36.8 to 32.8. RocketSim behaves as documented here (`arena/base.rs:1341` skips demolished cars in bumps), so nothing for `ROCKETSIM_NOTES.md`.

**Candidate freeze reference at `e09d0e8` (superseded: the review found unheld shells; kept for the record)** (`target/ref-freeze`, train and validation, default and aligned, all 60 + 60 convert). Against `target/ref-final` (`8acdb70`): default masked car and ball rows identical at all horizons and sizes on both splits; one-step car position 3.81 to 3.80 (train) and 3.90 to 3.88 UU (validation) at p90, ball velocity p99 396 to 387 and 402 to 393 UU/s; car one-step samples drop by 2 (train) and 6 (validation), the held dead shells. Aligned masked car improves at every horizon and size (validation h1 0.564 / 16.49 / 53.8 to 0.517 / 15.83 / 52.3 UU; the pooled exception is validation 1v1 h1 p99 69.0 to 70.3); aligned masked ball p90 within 0.25 UU. Per-replay p90 worse by more than 2%: 1 (train default), 1 (validation default), 12 (train aligned), 19 (validation aligned). The seven worse by more than 10% are all aligned ball rows: five follow the `earlier` rule alone or its interaction with the replay-wide offset estimate (the aligned target moves by about a tick; on some replays the estimate helps, on others it hurts); `2v2/00b3b6e3` is the spawn-pose window, still wrong in the aligned variant (h4 error 104 against 8.9 UU); validation `1v1/1a0aba95` is a non-lag change, not inspected (validation). Draft acceptance bands from these reports: "B itself" null mean 5.6, p95 12, p99 13 of 84 rows; cross-split selfcheck 4 and 4 outside. Section 5 numbers on validation: scoreboard 98.6% equal, 1.4% off by one, 0 by more; pads matched 24,116 of 24,117, path reaches the pad 96.6%; simulated touches inside a contact interval 97.3%, contact intervals holding a touch 84.7%; 128 non-goal demolitions (86 observed, 41 repeats, 1 unlinked), 455 post-goal explosions.

**Review fixes for `c5b85b9`** (reviewer). (1) Regression fixed: the demolition-coincident shell's sleeping start was suppressed whenever its demolition was reported in the frame, but the event handler only runs in simulated, non-withheld frames, so 26 of 191 such shells (post-goal and countdown frames, 11 train replays; e.g. `3v3/0005f298` frame 8910) were never held. The suppression now applies only when the handler runs; elsewhere the sleeping packet starts the hold (inferred). Over all 60 train replays: 440 fresh sleeping unlinked primary shells, none never held. (2) The spawn-pose hold is no longer released in a withheld frame (it lasts while the car has no body in the observations); an observed demolition of a spawn-held car is a normal demolition; and a previous dead shell's release on the same slot no longer clears the spawn hold's internal demolished state, which was why the `2v2/00b3b6e3` window stayed wrong in the aligned variant (ball error at frames 2201-2204 36 / 59 / 81 / 104 to 9.1 / 9.0 / 9.0 / 8.9 UU, the `f9e2c9f` values). Tests for each. `spawn_pose_held` is a typed Parquet column (per car slot, bool) returned by both loaders; new columns are appended and `columnar_version` stays 1. (3) `convert_replay` moves existing outputs to backups before publishing and restores them on failure (success path exercised; a rename failure could not be provoked). (4) Every replay-reading tool uses the shared sealed-path check (`read_replay_file`, `ensure_unsealed`; no `contains("test")` checks remain; diagnostic tools check the path they are given, the five protocol tools every file they open). Reviewer checks that passed: `dead_shell_held` marks exactly the held frames in both formats and loaders; the spawn-pose hold never affects a car that has a packet and costs under 1.5 UU at its last held frame in active play (p50 1.0 to 1.9 UU against the first packet).

**Review of `509c540`.** Verified: over all 60 train replays 440 fresh sleeping unlinked shells, 191 coincident with a demolition, none unheld; the backup publish leaves an existing export byte-identical when the main rename fails (forced with the old file held open), and leaves no backups after success. The spawn-pose hold's cost, measured as the exported position at the last spawn-held frame in active play against the first packet (179 cases on train; countdown cases identical): p50 / p90 / p99 1.0 / 2.0 / 3.0 UU when the car was simulated (`bd81e3a`), 1.9 / 2.9 / 4.3 (`c5b85b9`), 2.5 / 3.4 / 5.2 (`509c540`, the hold now also survives a previous shell's release). Accepted: about 1.5 UU on a few hundred frames against the double hits it removes (up to 104 UU on the ball); a kinematic advance of the held pose is a possible refinement. Residuals noted: a process killed between backup and publish leaves `.bak-<pid>` files and no main file under its name; `replay_audit` read every file under its root without a per-file sealed check (being fixed).

## Reference for the frozen build (2026-10-03)

`scripts/run_reference.sh` at `509c540` (`target/ref-freeze2/`; train and validation, default and aligned; 120 of 120 convert; wall time train 265 / 729 s, validation 263 / 723 s). Against the superseded `e09d0e8` run every pooled row and sample count is identical except the aligned train ball p99 (back to the `ref-final` level, e.g. h4 71.9 to 70.8 UU), and no replay is worse by more than 2%; the `2v2/00b3b6e3` spawn-pose window is resolved. Against `ref-final` (`8acdb70`) the summary of the `e09d0e8` run holds (default masked rows identical, aligned masked car better at every horizon and size, car one-step samples -2 / -6 for held dead shells). Still worse than `ref-final` by more than 10% per replay: train aligned ball `1v1/00a0da63` h1 (+22%), `3v3/00054e5d` h4 (+13%), `3v3/001312fe` h2 (+13%), all from the `earlier` rule alone or with the replay-wide offset estimate (the aligned target moves about a tick), and validation `3v3/1a1c2c0d`, `3v3/1a5a0073`, `1v1/1a0aba95` (not inspected). Acceptance bands from these four reports (`target/acceptance-bands.json`): the "B itself" null mean 5.5, p95 11, p99 13, max 14 of 84 rows; train bands on validation 3 outside, validation bands on train 2. The tip `a30470a` converts all 120 development replays to byte-identical default JSONL (SHA-256), so the reference stands for it. `TEST_PROTOCOL.md` is rewritten on this reference (section 4 table, section 5 numbers, stated limits).

## Test-split assessment (2026-10-03, run once)

Protocol: `TEST_PROTOCOL.md` as approved by the user on 2026-10-03; tag `test-assessment-1` = `99f0a9d`; section 3a run once, in order, by `target/test-run/run.sh` (outputs and logs in `target/test-run/`, reports `target/test-{default,aligned,aligned-raw,offline}.json`); no parameter, option or code was changed after any result. Host: the user's Windows workstation; wall time default 252 s, aligned 704 s, aligned raw predictor 500 s, offline fits 265 s (development: 263-265 s and 723-729 s).

**Robustness.** 60 of 60 test replays convert in every variant; every tool exited 0.

**Accuracy (pooled, p50 / p90 / p99; development validation value in brackets).** One-step car position before correction 0.04 / 3.5 / 35 UU (0.05 / 3.9 / 38; n 1,078,109), ball 0.01 / 0.0 / 20 (0.01 / 0.0 / 20); car one-step velocity 1.10 / 42.7 / 327 UU/s (1.45 / 48.6 / 345), rotation 0.24 / 2.9 / 10 deg (0.30 / 3.1 / 11), angular velocity 0.08 / 0.9 / 3 rad/s (0.09 / 0.9 / 3). Default masked car, 1 frame (start-packet age 0.067 s): simulated 16.0 / 40.0 / 75 UU, linear 17.4 / 43.0 / 75, hold 102 / 184 / 230 (16.6 / 41.0 / 75); 4 frames (0.167 s): 15.3 / 38.6 / 76, linear 25.8 / 61.3 / 131. Default masked ball, 1 frame (0.033 s): 10.0 / 34.1 / 63, linear 9.8 / 34.9 / 71 (10.7 / 33.1 / 66); 4 frames: 11.6 / 36.9 / 74, linear 13.5 / 52.8 / 300. Aligned masked car, 1 frame: 0.37 / 15.4 / 48, linear 5.9 / 22.4 / 63 (0.52 / 15.8 / 52); 4 frames 2.76 / 18.7 / 56. Aligned masked ball, 1 frame: 0.00 / 13.3 / 27 (0.00 / 13.1 / 28). Raw-predictor and offline-fit runs (context, not graded): raw-predictor masked car 1 frame 18.8 / 53.9 / 77 against linear 21.4 / 55.0 / 79; offline-fit one-step rotation 0.21 / 2.1 / 9 deg and angular velocity 0.06 / 0.5 / 2 rad/s (partly in-sample, as on development).

**Acceptance** (`scripts/acceptance.py check`, bands from the four reference reports of `target/ref-freeze2`). 84 comparisons, none skipped; **7 outside their band, within the empirical null for a fresh split (mean 5.5, p95 11, p99 13)**. Four are below the band (better than development): one-step car position p90 3v3 (2.52 against [2.73, 3.23]) and p99 pooled (36.95 against [37.25, 38.78]), one-step ball position p99 (15.71 against [15.77, 17.29]), aligned masked ball h3 p99 (28.7 against [28.9, 39.6]). Three are above, all in 1v1 masked car position at 1 frame: default p90 47.3 against [37.4, 42.3], default p99 73.3 against [51.1, 71.3], aligned p99 53.2 against [26.9, 52.0]; the 1v1 test replays are worse than development on this row (pooled over sizes it is in band: 43.3 against [38.3, 47.0]). Ordering rules: 30 graded, 0 broken. Outlier replays (own p90 above every development replay of the same size): `2v2/ffec9151` one-step car position 13.75 UU (development maximum 9.4), `2v2/fff83994` one-step car position 16.13 (9.4), `3v3/fff7a1ae` one-step car position 9.34 (8.45), `2v2/ffedf868` default masked car h1 57.5 (55.4); causes not examined in this run.

**Consistency checks** (test; development train / validation). Scoreboard: shown clock equals the ceiling of the reconstruction on 98.4% of 622,987 running frames, off by one 1.6%, never by more (98.4% / 98.6%). Boost pickups: 23,287, pad matched 100.0%, car path reaches the pad 96.2% (96.1% / 96.6%). Simulated touches inside a ball-contact interval 96.7% (97.0% / 97.3%); **ball-contact intervals holding a simulated touch 80.9% (84.9% / 84.7%)**, about 4 points lower than development, the largest gap among these checks. Non-goal demolition events 332 = 207 linked and not repeats + 107 repeats + 18 with no linked car; 260 post-goal explosions excluded; simulated demolitions 0 by construction. Lag-free replays: 0 of 60.

**Reading.** The test split behaves like the development splits: the pooled one-step and masked rows are at or slightly better than validation, the miss count is within what a fresh split shows by chance, and no ordering rule breaks. The one consistent weakness is 1v1 masked car position at 1 frame (three of the seven misses), and the contact-interval recall is 4 points below development. Both are generalisation findings to look into on train and validation, not by tuning on the test replays; the test split is now spent for unseen evaluation.

## Per-player ping, and `AirActivateCount` (2026-10-03)

Prompted by the comparison with the user's `rlgym-tools` / `rust-carball` converter, which reads both fields (evaluator; train for design, validation once as a check; scratch tools in `target/t12/`). **Ping** is `Engine.PlayerReplicationInfo:Ping` (a byte, probably ms/4, unverified). Every corpus player has one; the recording player's median byte is 7 (p10/p90 4/14), refreshed every 0.03-1 s when it jitters and every 10 s otherwise, like the others, so its cadence picks out the recorder in only 33 of 56 train and 34 of 49 validation replays: not a detector (the fitted ground shift is better). **Against the fitted ground-control shift:** the recorder's own cars have median shifts of 8.5 / 9 / 10 / 12 / 13 / 14 ticks at ping byte 0-4 / 4-7 / 7-10 / 10-14 / 14-20 / 20+ (train; validation 9 / 9 / 10 / 11 / 14 / 13); per replay Spearman 0.68 (train) and 0.52 (validation), explaining 16-34% of the between-replay variance (residual sd about 1.5 ticks); within a replay Spearman 0.14. Other cars stay at median 0 at every ping. LAN client replays: only the remote human has a ping (byte about 37); its fitted shift is 17 and 20 ticks and its true control lead over the host and the bots is about 31 ticks (signed delay median -23 against +8; game 2 -22 against +9). The **ball-car offset** does not relate to ping (Spearman -0.08 train, -0.10 validation). **Decision:** no converter change; the per-event fit already finds the shift, and a ping-based prior would only matter with a narrower search range. Ping is worth exporting as an observed per-player field. **`AirActivateCount`** (LAN game 1 host replay) is a 0-8 counter set loosely around jumps and dodges and reset around landings (a value-1 update follows a jump press within 6 ticks in 41% and a dodge start in 34%; a value-0 update follows a landing in 60%; values of 2 or more match nothing); it equals no flag and is not usable as an exact signal.

## Training labels and ping in the export (2026-10-03)

At the user's request the export carries convenience training labels apart from the state (README): `episode`, `episode_seconds_remaining`, `next_scoring_team`, `seconds_until_next_goal` (future-derived, from the observed goal events and the `Active` game state), `update_age_seconds` per car slot (observed), and in the header the replay's final score, winner, episode and goal counts from its own last scoreboard; plus the observed per-player `ping_raw`. Nothing feeds the conversion: the default JSONL of train `1v1/00a0da63` (13,413 lines, overtime) is identical line by line once the new keys are removed. Checks: on that replay 13 goals (7-6) give 14 episodes (13 goal-ended plus the tied regulation), every score increment matches `next_scoring_team` at its goal frame (0.0 s remaining); on 11 more train replays episodes equal the goals, or the goals plus one when the replay ends in play (7 of 11), and every goal pause starts on its goal event's frame; on the LAN game 1 host replay the score is 5-3 over 9 episodes and only the remote player has a ping (median raw 38; host and bots null). `verify_direct_parquet.py` re-derives the labels from the JSONL and matches the Parquet columns. Limits: goal and episode times are those of the frames that report them; a team whose score was never replicated reads 0 (source `inferred_match_start`); update ages are at frame resolution and null after a respawn until the new car's first packet. Not ported from `rlgym-tools`: discrete action matching (user decision).

**Packet freshness columns.** The export also says which bodies got a fresh packet in each frame (`ball_fresh`, `car_fresh`, by the converter's own test, the one the `packet_lags` records and the corrections use), the ball's frame-time update age, and the age of the last packet in server ticks (`ball_packet_age_ticks`, `car_packet_age_ticks`; offline lag inference, `default` lags counted as unknown). Check on train `1v1/00a0da63`: the default JSONL is unchanged once the new keys are removed; the columns equal an independent re-derivation from the JSONL; `car_fresh` has 10,148 slot-frames against 8,941 `packet_lags` car rows, the 1,207 extra being fresh packets in frames the converter does not simulate (goal pause 1,059, countdown 121, first kickoff frame 27); the tick age at a fresh frame is 0-4 for the ball in all 8,774 cases and for cars in 8,588 of 8,591 (three chain lags of 6, 6 and 7 ticks); the car tick age is known for 75.2% of slot-frames with a car. Per-control ages (throttle, steer, handbrake) were not added: those fields replicate only on change, so their age measures how long an input was held, not how stale the data is.

**Review of the label branch.** A reviewer found no leak (default JSONL of three train replays identical to `master` without the new keys) and no label error (all 60 train replays: 425 goals in `PostGoalScored` frames, goal counts per team equal each replay's final scoreboard, scoring direction checked against the score increment, overtime kickoffs open an episode in all 9 overtime replays). Fixed from its findings: the observed per-car update age moved from `labels` to `freshness` (`car_update_age_seconds`), so everything under `labels` / `label_*` is future-derived and each frame's `labels` says `future_derived: true`; `car_fresh` is null (not false) for a slot whose primary car cannot be resolved in the frame (30 slot-frames on one 2v2 train replay), and the numpy masks are int8 with -1 for unknown; `ping_age_seconds` per slot shows a carried-forward ping's age (median 2.3-4.2 s, maximum 10.0 s on four train replays); the docs now say the fresh masks are a superset of the `packet_lags` rows and that tick ages exceed 4 when a frame gap does (cars up to 9 on train).

## RocketSim `0b02051` to `9910c58` (2026-10-04)

Upstream `v3-rust` moved 9 commits; three touch the simulation: `ff54d6b` (boost forces full throttle only with all four wheels grounded; the sticky force uses the raw throttle), `3ddea3f` (transient wheel contacts reset on respawn) and `80c2f1f` (opt-in `reset_car_transient_contacts` and `clear_persistent_manifolds`, documented for planning code that teleports cars in a reused arena). The update builds without code changes; the converter got an opt-in `reset_scratch_contacts` (default off) that calls the two resets after every scratch-arena teleport of the offline fits, never in the main arena. Measured against `target/ref-freeze2` (A; `master`'s state is identical to `509c540`), the new revision (B) and B with the resets (C), from builds of `5fdeb08`:
* **B against A, train:** every pooled row unchanged to the printed precision (one-step car position 0.053 / 3.80 / 37.5 UU, car velocity p50 1.475 to 1.474 UU/s, ball velocity p99 387.1 to 386.7; masked car h4 p50 16.643 to 16.633 UU); per replay no row worse by more than 2% in the default variant, one in the aligned (`3v3/000e1a16` masked car h3 p90 17.23 to 17.87 UU). `error_budget` partitions, including ground boosting (92,508 residuals) and wall/ramp boosting (10,543), unchanged to the printed precision: the throttle change rarely matters because the converter's boosting cars mostly have full observed throttle and the old code already forced the engine throttle when throttle was 0.
* **B against A, validation:** pooled rows unchanged (car velocity p50 1.451 to 1.449 UU/s); per replay one default row worse by more than 2% (`1v1/1a0ad1c2` masked car h2 p90 +2.1%) and two aligned (`1v1/1a0ad1c2` h2 +11.7%, `3v3/1a1c065f` h3 +6.0%), small-sample tails, not inspected (validation).
* **LAN server truth** (both games, client and host): ball rows identical, car rows equal to rounding (e.g. game 1 client frames without a fresh packet position p50 1.00 to 1.01 UU), flags within single frames.
* **C against B, train:** neutral (one-step car velocity p50 1.474 to 1.479 UU/s, aligned masked car h1 p90 16.41 to 16.39 UU; per replay one aligned row worse by more than 2%, `2v2/00b0c3e7` h3 and h4 +2.3%); LAN equal to rounding. Left off.
* **`ROCKETSIM_NOTES.md` reproductions:** same numbers as at `0b02051` (no status change).
Decision: adopt `9910c58` (no regression; respawn contact handling and API kept current); `reset_scratch_contacts` stays an option, off.

## Release clean-up (2026-10-04)

Branch `cleanup-release` from `master` (tagged `pre-cleanup`, which keeps every removed tool and option). Removed: 41 one-off experiment binaries and 16 analysis scripts whose results are recorded above; the measured-and-rejected options (low-air angular holds, the hit-impulse workaround for RocketSim before `0b02051`, the packet-interval control rule, boost-pickup lookahead, the scratch-contact reset, the alternative flip-cancel sources, the hand-tuned air-persistence gates, the legacy non-exact lag chains, `lag_boundary`); the always-on feature switches, now plain code; the tools' ablation flags (the evaluator keeps the eight its protocol and README use); unused diagnostic counters and tracing. `ConvertOptions` went from 63 fields to 22; the four input-timing fits share `input_fits`; numeric knobs are constants. `conversion.rs` is split into `conversion/{mod,packet_lags,fits,air}.rs`; the code is `cargo fmt`-formatted and clippy-clean (four structural lints allowed in `Cargo.toml` with reasons) and the API docs build without warnings. MIT license, README rewritten, field reference in `docs/output-format.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, version 1.0.0. Verification: the default JSONL of all 120 development replays, converted before and after, has byte-identical frames and headers identical apart from the options list (checked after the removals, after the options rework, and on the final tree); on the final tree 7 headers differed in the run made while the test suite ran alongside, and all 7 matched the reference when re-run alone, as did repeated runs of both builds (not reproduced; frames identical in every run); `cargo test --all-targets` and the Python tests pass. The test-split result therefore still describes the converter's state.


## Output size and conversion cost (2026-10-06)

For the v2 design (`docs/v2-plan.md`); no converter change. Three train replays, one per game size (`1v1/0000a984`, 0.83 MB; `2v2/000c0390`, 0.75 MB; `3v3/00054e5d`, 1.61 MB), converted to Parquet with the release `convert_replay` at `5a7da58` (RocketSim `9910c58`), on the development machine. Reproduce: `python scripts/measure_output_size.py <export.parquet>...` (PyArrow 25.0.1; nothing is written) and, from `scripts/stage_timing/`, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=../../target cargo run --release -- <replay>...`.

**Where the bytes go.** The main file is 12.45 / 12.09 / 28.14 MB (15.0x / 16.1x / 17.5x the replay); the seven record tables add 0.17 / 0.2 / 0.32 MB. `frame_json` is 72% of the main file (8.85 / 8.64 / 20.19 MB). The typed columns alone are 3.61 / 3.48 / 7.55 MB, of which the car rotation matrices are the largest (0.96 / 1.16 / 2.92 MB).

| Re-encoding of the typed columns (PyArrow) | 1v1 | 2v2 | 3v3 |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| without `frame_json`, zstd 3 | 3.61 | 3.48 | 7.55 |
| + rotations as quaternions (size proxy) | 2.69 | 2.58 | 5.83 |
| + BYTE_STREAM_SPLIT on floats | 1.79 | 1.77 | 4.05 |
| + zstd 9 | 1.75 | 1.72 | 3.95 |
| + zstd 19 | 1.65 | 1.66 | 3.79 |

What only `frame_json` holds, as typed columns (zstd 9, split floats): the other car-state fields (timers, flags, previous controls) 0.22 / 0.27 / 0.56 MB; the observations with their update frames 0.92 / 1.13 / 2.75 MB; the position residuals (16,971 / 15,520 / 35,932 rows) 1.23 / 1.17 / 2.69 MB. A lossless state file of the v2 kind is then about 1.97 / 1.99 / 4.51 MB (2.4x / 2.6x / 2.8x the replay). Car state quantized to integers (0.01 UU, 0.01 UU/s, 1e-4 rad/s, rotation 1/30000) with delta encoding in a car-sorted layout: 0.55 / 0.63 / 1.52 MB against 0.94 / 1.11 / 2.72 MB as split floats (lossy). Limits: the quaternion figure uses component magnitudes (signs not resolved), so it is a size estimate; one replay per size.

Frames by clock state: running 77.0 / 80.2 / 84.4%, countdown 10.0 / 8.9 / 6.6%, goal pause 6.9 / 6.6 / 4.1%, kickoff 5.6 / 4.3 / 3.2%, expired 0.6 / 0 / 1.7%.

**Where the time goes** (one run each, wall time): parse 0.01 / 0.00 / 0.01 s, observation extraction 0.02 / 0.02 / 0.04 s, packet-lag inference 0.04 / 0.01 / 0.02 s, conversion with those lags and no fits (`input_fits`, `air_bvp`, `infer_air_controls_from_lookahead`, `align_contacts` off) 0.18 / 0.15 / 0.32 s, the same with the air lookahead on 0.16 / 0.15 / 0.31 s, the full default 2.32 / 2.41 / 5.45 s. The fits are 90-95% of a conversion; RocketSim and the bookkeeping 34,000-65,000 frames per second.

## v2 encodings: rotation and lossy state (2026-10-06)

For `docs/v2-plan.md`; no converter change. The same three train exports as the section above, re-encoded by `python scripts/prototype_v2_encodings.py <export.parquet>...` (PyArrow 25.0.1, zstd 9, per column the better of plain and BYTE_STREAM_SPLIT; lossy variants also with per-series delta encoding). The physics columns are ball and car position, velocity, angular velocity and rotation; "rest" is every other typed v1 column except `frame_json` (0.44 / 0.39 / 0.83 MB); the car internals measured above (0.22 / 0.27 / 0.56 MB) are added to the totals.

| Physics encoding | 1v1 | 2v2 | 3v3 | File with rest and internals (1v1 / 2v2 / 3v3) |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| float32, rotation 3x3 matrix (v1) | 1.68 | 1.72 | 4.11 | 2.34 / 2.38 / 5.49 MB |
| float32, quaternion (Shepperd, w >= 0) | 1.18 | 1.23 | 2.95 | 1.85 / 1.90 / 4.34 |
| float32, forward and up columns | 1.38 | 1.42 | 3.38 | 2.05 / 2.08 / 4.77 |
| integers: 0.01 UU, 0.01 UU/s, 1e-4 rad/s, quaternion x32767 (int16) | 0.65 | 0.66 | 1.50 | 1.31 / 1.33 / 2.88 |
| integers: 0.1 UU, 0.1 UU/s, 1e-3 rad/s, quaternion x32767 | 0.55 | 0.56 | 1.25 | 1.21 / 1.23 / 2.64 |

Rotation alone (cars): matrix 0.61 / 0.71 / 1.78 MB, quaternion 0.26 / 0.31 / 0.78 MB; the matrix rebuilt from the quaternion differs from the exported one by at most 9.1e-7 per element. The fine integers have maximum errors 0.005 UU, 0.005 UU/s and 5e-5 rad/s and save 30-34% of the quaternion file; per-series delta beats interleaved integers by 12-23%. The coarse level saves 7-8% more. Projected to 140,000 replays at the development corpus's mean replay size (1.23 MB): about 380-460 GB lossless with quaternions, 280-310 GB with the fine integers (v1 Parquet about 2.8 TB). Limits: one replay per game size; the v2 "rest" columns will differ from v1's.

**RocketSim's rlpr recordings, for comparison** (`rocketsim_test/src/rlpr/` at `9910c58`): per 120 Hz tick, one raw `repr(C)` record per car (604 bytes in format v9, with wheel suspension, contact and impulse records) and one for the ball, behind a magic, an endianness flag and a version that gates later fields (absent ones get sentinels such as `TOUCH_FRAME_UNKNOWN`); the whole file is zstd-compressed (the bundled 300 s recordings: 16 MB 2v2, 29-31 MB 3v3). Its replay mode (`examples/rlpr_replay/mod.rs`, `ReplayPlan`) drives each tick's recorded controls and restores state only at discontinuities (kickoff, demolition respawn).

## RocketSim `9910c58` to crates.io 0.2.7 (2026-10-07)

Story 0.1 of `docs/v2-plan.md`. The latest compatible RocketSim is the published crate: crates.io `rocketsim` 0.2.7 has the same `src/` as `rocketsim/` at the `v3-rust` tip (`dc5d60e`, 2026-10-06; compared ignoring line endings; `dc5d60e` itself changes only the test crate), so the dependency is now `rocketsim = "=0.2.7"` and no longer blocks a crates.io release. Upstream moved 10 commits from `9910c58`; the ones that touch the simulation: suspension strengths from the mass distribution (`9f2667f`), wheel impulses applied inside the raycast loop (`6197beb`), throttle and steer quantized where forces use them (`8803d52`), dodge direction quantized like air control (`f4a5d42`), the dodge deadzone separated from the flip torque direction (`ea35505`), and the boost pad AABB sized by the cylinder radius (`26c48d3`). The converter builds without code changes; `cargo test --all-targets` passes.

Protocol as for the previous update: A = `git archive` of `d60b133` (RocketSim `9910c58`, built in `target/rs-a/`), B = the same tree with 0.2.7; `evaluate_corpus` default and `--aligned-targets` on train and validation, `error_budget` on train, `rlbot_reconstruction` on the four LAN-truth replays, and the `ROCKETSIM_NOTES.md` reproductions, all from `target/rs-compare/run.sh` and `notes.sh` (ignored). Per-replay comparison: `python scripts/compare_reports.py A.json B.json` (new; p90 of every `simulated` row of each replay, flagged when worse by more than 2% from a value of at least 0.5).
* **Pooled rows:** unchanged to the printed precision in all four reports (train default: car one-step position 0.05 / 3.8 / 38 UU, car velocity p50 1.47 to 1.48 UU/s, ball velocity p99 387 to 388 UU/s, masked car h4 p50 16.63 UU; validation default: car velocity p50 1.45 to 1.46 UU/s, the rest identical; aligned masked car h1 0.56 / 16.4 / 55 UU train and 0.52 / 15.8 / 52 UU validation in both). Sample counts identical.
* **Per replay** (2,400 p90 rows per report): default train 361 better / 337 worse, beyond 2% 1 / 4; default validation 324 / 330, beyond 2% 3 / 1; aligned train 431 / 382, beyond 2% 4 / 3; aligned validation 374 / 375, beyond 2% 7 / 6. Every row beyond 2% in either direction is a masked-kinematics row (about 90 samples per replay); largest worse `validation/3v3/1a1e8a14` default h1 car angular velocity p90 1.084 to 1.170 rad/s (+7.9%). Not inspected (no pattern; validation).
* **`error_budget` (train):** every partition equal to the printed precision except the last digit of a few columns (e.g. air boosting 57,605 to 57,607 residuals).
* **LAN server truth:** car and ball rows equal to the printed precision; flags within single frames (game 1 client: `is_on_ground` mismatches 410 to 408 of 22,922); input-error shares within 0.1 points.
* **`ROCKETSIM_NOTES.md` reproductions:** same to the printed precision apart from last digits; the flipping car still reports |w| 7.45 rad/s (entry 2 unchanged); the 2,000 UU/s hit probe still sends the ball out at 2,662 UU/s; `diagnose_contacts` phantom hits 1,807 to 1,806, misses 943 unchanged; `audit_tick_integrality` and `audit_car_tick_integrality` identical.
Decision: adopt crates.io 0.2.7 (no regression; current upstream; publishable dependency). The v1 reference for the v2 parity work is B.

## v2: determinism of v1, workspace, and the decode stage (2026-10-07)

Stories 0.2, 0.3, 1.1, 1.2, 2.1 and 2.2 of `docs/v2-plan.md`.

**Reference and determinism.** `v1-final` tags `9e55dc7` (v1 on RocketSim 0.2.7); its `evaluate_corpus` reports are the B reports of the section above (copied to the ignored `target/ref-v1-final/`). Every train and validation replay converted three times to default JSONL by that build, 16 conversions at a time (`target/determinism/run.sh`, ignored; outputs hashed and deleted): headers and frames byte-identical in all three runs for all 120 replays. The 7 header differences seen once during the release clean-up (RESULTS.md, "Release clean-up") still do not reproduce. One source of run-to-run variation exists in the code without reaching the output: `infer_packet_lags` builds the car runs by iterating a `std::collections::HashMap` (`conversion/packet_lags.rs:699`), so their order and indices differ between runs; v2 iterates in car-life order.

**Workspace.** The repository is a Cargo workspace: v1 stays at the root as package `replicar-v1` with library `replicar_v1` (renamed from `replicar` so that v2's library can take the name; v1's binaries and tests changed only their `replicar::` paths), and v2 lives in `crates/`: `replicar-format` (frames, ticks, players, teams; no parser or simulator), `replicar` (the library) and `replicar-eval` (parity and evaluation tools, depending on v1 by path). Dependency pins are workspace dependencies. RocketSim's re-export of glam lacks `Quat`, so v2 keeps the direct `glam` pin (the same version, unified).

**Decode** (`crates/replicar/src/decode/`: network property names as constants, the actor tracker, typed values with their frames, `GameState`, `DemolitionReport`, `NetworkEvent`, `CarLife`, `PlayerKey`). Parity: `cargo run --release -p replicar-eval --bin parity -- decode replays/train replays/validation` maps v2's decoded feed into v1's `ObservedReplay` (`replicar-eval/src/v1_shape.rs`) and compares the two serialized: **120 of 120 replays equal** (every frame, car, player, event, pad record and diagnostic).

## v2: update-tick inference (2026-10-07)

Story 3.1 of `docs/v2-plan.md`: `crates/replicar/src/update_ticks/` (v1's `packet_lags.rs`): whole-tick chains (`chains.rs`), the ball's free flight, bridged hits and placement against the cars (`ball.rs`), lag-free detection, and the result as `UpdateTicks` (ticks before each update's frame, by car life and frame). The hitbox table moved into the crate (`crates/replicar/data/body_hitboxes.tsv`, a copy of `data/`, so that the crate can be published) behind a `Hitbox` enum. Car runs are built in car-life order (v1: `HashMap` order). Unit tests on a synthetic replay (whole-tick updates recovered exactly, half-tick offsets rejected, no chain across a withheld frame; a bridged hit recovers its elapsed ticks). Parity: `parity update_ticks replays/train replays/validation` compares v1's `infer_packet_lags` (default options) with v2's in a canonical order (runs sorted by car life and first frame; each update's run named by that identity): **120 of 120 replays equal**, including ball-car offsets, bridged hits, lag-free detection and every run's feasible range and start.
